The Temporal Trajectory of Visual Perspective in Autobiographical Memory

To fix (3)

To fix Ethics Approval Check

What we found

1 of 1 paper appeared to involve live data collection and lacked an ethics approval statement.

Show details

Based on the following text, we would expect an ethics approval statement, but it was not present:

After providing informed consent, participants completed an AM retrieval task.

All memories were required to have occurred within the past five years and to include events in which participants were personally involved.

For each memory, participants provided a unique title and an event date.

When the exact day was unknown, participants were instructed to estimate the date by indicating the beginning (day 1), middle (day 15), or end (day 28) of the month.

Before providing these ratings, participants were given definitions of both perspectives, explaining that own-eyes memories are experienced from the original viewpoint of the self, whereas observer memories involve viewing the event from an external vantage point in which the self can be seen.

Participants were informed that memories could be experienced exclusively from one perspective or fluctuate between perspectives.

To fix Exact P-Values

What we found

We found 2 imprecise p values out of 22 detected p values.

Show details

Reporting p values imprecisely (e.g., p < .05) reduces transparency, reproducibility, and re-use (e.g., in p value meta-analyses). Best practice is to report exact p-values with three decimal places (e.g., p = .032) unless p values are smaller than 0.001, in which case you can use p < .001.

This module sometimes flags false positives when sentences discuss how p-value thresholds were set. It can also miss an imprecise p-value that is reported against a threshold (e.g., “decreased at the p < .05 significance threshold”).

P-Value Text
ps > .33 Between-person differences in reliving, rehearsal, and emotional intensity were not significantly associated with either perspective (all ps > .33; see Table S8 for full statistics).
ps > .35 All differences were non-significant (all ps > .35) and all 95% CIs for d included zero.

The APA manual states: Report exact p values (e.g., p = .031) to two or three decimal places. However, report p values less than .001 as p < .001. However, 2 decimals is too imprecise for many use-cases (e.g., a p value meta-analysis), so report p values with three digits.

American Psychological Association (2020). Publication manual of the American Psychological Association, 7 edition. American Psychological Association.

To check (4)

To check Non-Significant P Value Check

What we found

We found 6 non-significant p values that should be checked for appropriate interpretation.

Show details

Meta-scientific research has shown nonsignificant p values are commonly misinterpreted. It is incorrect to infer that there is ‘no effect’, ‘no difference’, or that groups are ‘the same’ after p > 0.05.

It is possible that there is a true non-zero effect, but that the study did not detect it. Make sure your inference acknowledges that it is possible that there is a non-zero effect. It is correct to include the effect is ‘not significantly’ different, although this just restates that p > 0.05.

Metacheck does not yet analyze automatically whether sentences which include non-significant p-values are correct, but we recommend manually checking the sentences below for possible misinterpreted non-significant p values.

Text Sentence
p = .087 In the observer model, the within-person rehearsal coefficient was not significant at the reference memory age (β = -0.034, p = .087), but its interaction with log memory age was negative and significant (β = -0.012, p = .035), indicating that the association between rehearsal and observer ratings became more negative as memory age increased.
ps > .33 Between-person differences in reliving, rehearsal, and emotional intensity were not significantly associated with either perspective (all ps > .33; see Table S8 for full statistics).
p = .269 The interaction was not significant (β = 0.011, SE = 0.010, t(34,813) = 1.11, p = .269).
p = .49 Model diagnostics confirmed an adequate basis dimension for the smooth term in both perspectives, with non-significant k-index tests (Observer: k-index = 1.01, p = .49; Own-Eyes: k-index = 1.00, p = .41).
p = .41 Model diagnostics confirmed an adequate basis dimension for the smooth term in both perspectives, with non-significant k-index tests (Observer: k-index = 1.01, p = .49; Own-Eyes: k-index = 1.00, p = .41).
ps > .35 All differences were non-significant (all ps > .35) and all 95% CIs for d included zero.

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate of misinterpretations of non-significant results is high, see:

Aczel B, Palfi B, Szollosi A, Kovacs M, Szaszi B, Szecsi P, Zrubka M, Gronau Q, van den Bergh D, Wagenmakers E (2018). “Quantifying Support for the Null Hypothesis in Psychology: An Empirical Investigation.” Advances in Methods and Practices in Psychological Science, 1(3), 357–366. doi:10.1177/2515245918773742.

Murphy S, Merz R, Reimann L, Fernández A (2025). “Nonsignificance misinterpreted as an effect’s absence in psychology: Prevalence and temporal analyses.” Royal Society Open Science, 12(3), 242167. doi:10.1098/rsos.242167.

For educational material on preventing the misinterpretation of p values, see Improving Your Statistical Inferences.

Code Comments

Missing Files

Absolute Paths

Best programming practice is to use relative file paths (e.g., ‘./files’) instead of absolute file paths (e.g., ‘C://Lakens/project_dir/files’) as these folder names do not exist on other computers. No absolute file paths were found in any of the code files.

Working Directory (setwd)

Best programming practice is to avoid setwd() in analysis code: it hardcodes an assumption about the working directory (often an absolute path on the author’s own machine), so the code breaks when run anywhere else. Keep the working directory as the caller sets it and use relative paths. No setwd() calls were found in any of the code files.

Package Installation (install.packages)

Best programming practice is to avoid install.packages() in analysis code: it installs software on whoever runs the script without asking, which is at minimum disruptive (an unexpected install, network access, a package version the author never tested against) and at worst a security risk if it points at an untrusted repository. Document required packages instead (a README, or a list of them: an renv.lock/DESCRIPTION for R, a requirements.txt for Python). No install.packages() calls were found in any of the code files.

Libraries / Imports

Best programming practice is to load all required libraries/imports in one block near the top of the code. In 2 code files, libraries/imports were at multiple places (i.e., with more than 3 non-comment lines in between).

Packages / Dependencies

The code files load 21 distinct packages/libraries: cowplot, dplyr, effectsize, emmeans, ggeffects, ggplot2, gratia, knitr, lme4, lmerTest, Matrix, mgcv, patchwork, performance, plyr, psych, readxl, reshape2, splines, tidyr, vegan. These are the names found in the source (no version information is available from static analysis).

Reproducible Environment

No renv.lock file, sessionInfo()/session_info() record, or groundhog/checkpoint date-pin call was found anywhere in the repository. Without one of these, the exact R and package versions used for the analysis are not recoverable, and package versions may drift between when this analysis was run and any later reproduction attempt. Spreadsheet formatting such as colour, merged cells, and blank rows/columns is not preserved when data are read programmatically or exported to CSV. Store data as a plain rectangular table (one header row, one column per variable, no colour-encoded meaning) so it is machine-readable.

Careless Responding

No file could be screened for careless responding.

What this does not cover. Only 0 of 1 data file could be screened: the rest had no detectable multi-item scale block, or too few respondents. Even in a screened file, these checks only look at multi-item rating scales — the only place where a repeated or flat answer pattern is interpretable. Single questions, open text, demographics and any item outside a detected scale block are never examined. A respondent can therefore answer most of a survey carelessly and still not be flagged here, and finding nothing is not evidence that a dataset is free of careless responding.

> make_ttest_row <- function(var_name, ttest_obj, d_obj) {
>   data.frame(
>     Variable  = var_name,
>     M_Study1  = round(ttest_obj$estimate[1], 2),
>     M_Study2  = round(ttest_obj$estimate[2], 2),
>     t         = round(ttest_obj$statistic,   2),
>     df        = round(ttest_obj$parameter,    0),
>     p         = round(ttest_obj$p.value,      3),
>     d         = round(abs(d_obj$Cohens_d),    2)
>   )
> }

> format_beta <- function(x, digits = 3) {
>   rounded <- round(x, digits)
>   ifelse(rounded == 0 & x != 0,
>          formatC(x, format = "e", digits = 2),
>          formatC(rounded, format = "f", digits = digits))
> }
> format_se <- function(x, digits = 3) {
>   rounded <- round(x, digits)
>   ifelse(rounded != 0, formatC(rounded, format = "f", digits = digits),
>          ifelse(x < 1e-6, formatC(x, format = "e", digits = 2), "<.001"))
> }
> get_model_rows <- function(model, model_label, predictor_labels, r2_marginal, r2_conditional) {
>   cf <- as.data.frame(coef(summary(model)))
>   r2_marginal    <- unname(r2_marginal)
>   r2_conditional <- unname(r2_conditional)
>   out <- data.frame(
>     Model       = c(model_label, rep("", nrow(cf) - 1)),
>     Predictor   = c("Intercept", predictor_labels),
>     beta        = format_beta(cf$Estimate),
>     SE          = format_se(cf$`Std. Error`),
>     df          = round(cf$df, 0),
>     t           = round(cf$`t value`, 2),
>     p           = ifelse(cf$`Pr(>|t|)` < .001, "<.001",
>                          as.character(round(cf$`Pr(>|t|)`, 3))),
>     R2_marginal    = c(round(r2_marginal, 3), rep(NA, nrow(cf) - 1)),
>     R2_conditional = c(round(r2_conditional, 3), rep(NA, nrow(cf) - 1))
>   )
>   rownames(out) <- NULL
>   out
> }
> obs_linear_rows <- get_model_rows(model_linear_fit, "Linear",
>                                    "Memory Age (c)",
>                                    r2_linear_obs$R2_marginal, r2_linear_obs$R2_conditional)
> obs_log_rows    <- get_model_rows(model_log_fit, "Logarithmic",

> data_clean <- data_clean %>%
>   group_by(ID) %>%
>   mutate(
>     Reliving_mean    = mean(Reliving,  na.rm = TRUE),
>     Reliving_within  = Reliving  - Reliving_mean,
>     Rehearsal_mean   = mean(Rehearsal, na.rm = TRUE),
>     Rehearsal_within = Rehearsal - Rehearsal_mean,
>     Intensity_mean   = mean(Intensity, na.rm = TRUE),
>     Intensity_within = Intensity - Intensity_mean

>   ungroup() %>%

>     Reliving_between  = Reliving_mean  - mean(Reliving_mean,  na.rm = TRUE),

> get_rows <- function(model) {
>   cf <- as.data.frame(coef(summary(model)))[-1, ]  # drop intercept
>   data.frame(
>     beta = round(cf$Estimate,     3),
>     SE   = round(cf$`Std. Error`, 3),
>     p    = ifelse(cf$`Pr(>|t|)` < .001, "<.001",
>                   as.character(round(cf$`Pr(>|t|)`, 3)))
>   )
> }
> predictor_labels <- c("Log(Memory Age)", "Characteristic (within-person)",
>                       "Characteristic (between-person)",
>                       "Log(MemAge) x Characteristic (within)")
> make_block <- function(model_obs, model_own, section_label) {
>   header_row <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = section_label,
>     obs_beta = NA_real_, obs_p = NA_character_,
>     own_beta = NA_real_, own_p = NA_character_
>   )
>   obs_block <- get_rows(model_obs)
>   own_block <- get_rows(model_own)
>   data_rows <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = predictor_labels,
>     obs_beta  = obs_block$beta,
>     obs_p     = obs_block$p,
>     own_beta  = own_block$beta,
>     own_p     = own_block$p
>   )
>   r2_obs <- r2(model_obs)
>   r2_own <- r2(model_own)
>   r2_row <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = "R2m / R2c",
>     obs_beta  = NA_real_,
>     obs_p     = paste0(round(r2_obs$R2_marginal, 3), " / ", round(r2_obs$R2_conditional, 3)),
>     own_beta  = NA_real_,
>     own_p     = paste0(round(r2_own$R2_marginal, 3), " / ", round(r2_own$R2_conditional, 3))
>   )
>   bind_rows(header_row, data_rows, r2_row)
> }
> combined_table <- bind_rows(
>   make_block(model_obs_reliving_final,  model_own_reliving_final,  "Reliving"),
>   make_block(model_obs_rehearsal_final, model_own_rehearsal_final, "Rehearsal"),
>   make_block(model_obs_intensity_final, model_own_intensity_final, "Intensity")
> )

> sd_low_col  <- "#7B2D8B"
> sd_mean_col <- "#2D9B6F"
> sd_high_col <- "#C0392B"
> label_sd_groups <- function(pred_obj) {
>   as.data.frame(pred_obj) %>%
>     mutate(Level = case_when(
>       as.numeric(as.character(group)) < 0 ~ "-1 SD",
>       as.numeric(as.character(group)) > 0 ~ "+1 SD",
>       TRUE                                ~ "Mean"
>     ),
>     Level = factor(Level, levels = c("-1 SD", "Mean", "+1 SD")))
> }
> get_legend_safe <- function(gg_plot) {
>   g <- ggplotGrob(gg_plot)
>   idx <- which(sapply(g$grobs, function(x) x$name) %in% c("guide-box", "guide-box-top"))
>   if (length(idx) == 0) return(NULL)
>   g$grobs[[idx[1]]]
> }
> make_int_plot <- function(df, title, y_label) {
>   ggplot(df, aes(x = x, y = predicted, color = Level, fill = Level)) +
>     geom_ribbon(aes(ymin = conf.low, ymax = conf.high),
>                 alpha = 0.15, color = NA, show.legend = FALSE) +
>     geom_line(linewidth = 1) +
>     scale_color_manual(values = c("-1 SD" = sd_low_col,
>                                   "Mean"  = sd_mean_col,
>                                   "+1 SD" = sd_high_col)) +
>     scale_fill_manual(values  = c("-1 SD" = sd_low_col,
>                                   "Mean"  = sd_mean_col,
>                                   "+1 SD" = sd_high_col),
>                        guide = "none") +
>     scale_y_continuous(limits = c(1, 7), breaks = 1:7) +
>     labs(title  = title,
>          x      = "Log Memory Age (centered)",
>          y      = y_label,
>          color  = NULL) +
>     theme_classic(base_size = 12) +
>     theme(plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5, size = 12),
>           axis.title       = element_text(size = 11),
>           axis.text        = element_text(size = 10),
>           legend.position  = "none",
>           plot.background  = element_rect(fill = "white", color = NA),
>           panel.background = element_rect(fill = "white", color = NA))
> }

> compare_coef <- function(full_model, sens_model, label) {
>   cf_full <- coef(summary(full_model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   cf_sens <- coef(summary(sens_model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   data.frame(
>     Outcome = label,
>     Sample  = c("Full (MemAge = 0 recoded to 0.5)", "MemAge = 0 excluded"),
>     beta    = round(c(cf_full["Estimate"], cf_sens["Estimate"]), 4),
>     SE      = round(c(cf_full["Std. Error"], cf_sens["Std. Error"]), 4),
>     p       = c(
>       ifelse(cf_full["Pr(>|t|)"] < .001, "<.001", as.character(round(cf_full["Pr(>|t|)"], 3))),
>       ifelse(cf_sens["Pr(>|t|)"] < .001, "<.001", as.character(round(cf_sens["Pr(>|t|)"], 3)))
>     )
>   )
> }
> sens_table <- bind_rows(
>   compare_coef(model_log_RS_final, model_log_RS_sens, "Observer"),
>   compare_coef(model2_log_RS_final, model2_log_RS_sens, "Own-Eyes")
> )
> knitr::kable(sens_table, row.names = FALSE,

> get_study_row <- function(model, outcome_label, study_label) {
>   cf <- coef(summary(model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   data.frame(
>     Outcome = outcome_label,
>     Study   = study_label,
>     beta    = round(cf["Estimate"], 4),
>     SE      = round(cf["Std. Error"], 4),
>     t       = round(cf["t value"], 2),
>     p       = ifelse(cf["Pr(>|t|)"] < .001, "<.001", as.character(round(cf["Pr(>|t|)"], 3)))
>   )
> }

> make_ttest_row <- function(var_name, ttest_obj, d_obj) {
>   data.frame(
>     Variable  = var_name,
>     M_Study1  = round(ttest_obj$estimate[1], 2),

>     p         = round(ttest_obj$p.value,      3),

>   )
> }

> format_beta <- function(x, digits = 3) {
>   rounded <- round(x, digits)
>   ifelse(rounded == 0 & x != 0,
>          formatC(x, format = "e", digits = 2),
>          formatC(rounded, format = "f", digits = digits))
> }
> format_se <- function(x, digits = 3) {
>   rounded <- round(x, digits)
>   ifelse(rounded != 0, formatC(rounded, format = "f", digits = digits),
>          ifelse(x < 1e-6, formatC(x, format = "e", digits = 2), "<.001"))
> }
> get_model_rows <- function(model, model_label, predictor_labels, r2_marginal, r2_conditional) {
>   cf <- as.data.frame(coef(summary(model)))
>   r2_marginal    <- unname(r2_marginal)
>   r2_conditional <- unname(r2_conditional)
>   out <- data.frame(
>     Model       = c(model_label, rep("", nrow(cf) - 1)),
>     Predictor   = c("Intercept", predictor_labels),
>     beta        = format_beta(cf$Estimate),
>     SE          = format_se(cf$`Std. Error`),
>     df          = round(cf$df, 0),
>     t           = round(cf$`t value`, 2),
>     p           = ifelse(cf$`Pr(>|t|)` < .001, "<.001",
>                          as.character(round(cf$`Pr(>|t|)`, 3))),
>     R2_marginal    = c(round(r2_marginal, 3), rep(NA, nrow(cf) - 1)),
>     R2_conditional = c(round(r2_conditional, 3), rep(NA, nrow(cf) - 1))
>   )
>   rownames(out) <- NULL
>   out
> }
> obs_linear_rows <- get_model_rows(model_linear_fit, "Linear",
>                                    "Memory Age (c)",
>                                    r2_linear_obs$R2_marginal, r2_linear_obs$R2_conditional)
> obs_log_rows    <- get_model_rows(model_log_fit, "Logarithmic",
>                                    "Log(Memory Age) (c)",
>                                    r2_log_obs$R2_marginal, r2_log_obs$R2_conditional)
> obs_poly_rows   <- get_model_rows(model_poly_fit, "Quadratic",
>                                    c("Memory Age (c)", "Memory Age (c)^2"),

> data_clean <- data_clean %>%
>   group_by(ID) %>%
>   mutate(
>     Reliving_mean    = mean(Reliving,  na.rm = TRUE),
>     Reliving_within  = Reliving  - Reliving_mean,

>     Rehearsal_within = Rehearsal - Rehearsal_mean,
>     Intensity_mean   = mean(Intensity, na.rm = TRUE),
>     Intensity_within = Intensity - Intensity_mean
>   ) %>%
>   ungroup() %>%
>   mutate(
>     Reliving_between  = Reliving_mean  - mean(Reliving_mean,  na.rm = TRUE),
>     Rehearsal_between = Rehearsal_mean - mean(Rehearsal_mean, na.rm = TRUE),
>     Intensity_between = Intensity_mean - mean(Intensity_mean, na.rm = TRUE)

> get_rows <- function(model) {
>   cf <- as.data.frame(coef(summary(model)))[-1, ]  # drop intercept
>   data.frame(
>     beta = round(cf$Estimate,     3),
>     SE   = round(cf$`Std. Error`, 3),
>     p    = ifelse(cf$`Pr(>|t|)` < .001, "<.001",
>                   as.character(round(cf$`Pr(>|t|)`, 3)))
>   )
> }
> predictor_labels <- c("Log(Memory Age)", "Characteristic (within-person)",
>                       "Characteristic (between-person)",
>                       "Log(MemAge) x Characteristic (within)")
> make_block <- function(model_obs, model_own, section_label) {
>   header_row <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = section_label,
>     obs_beta = NA_real_, obs_p = NA_character_,
>     own_beta = NA_real_, own_p = NA_character_
>   )
>   obs_block <- get_rows(model_obs)
>   own_block <- get_rows(model_own)
>   data_rows <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = predictor_labels,
>     obs_beta  = obs_block$beta,
>     obs_p     = obs_block$p,
>     own_beta  = own_block$beta,
>     own_p     = own_block$p
>   )
>   r2_obs <- r2(model_obs)
>   r2_own <- r2(model_own)
>   r2_row <- data.frame(
>     Predictor = "R2m / R2c",
>     obs_beta  = NA_real_,
>     obs_p     = paste0(round(r2_obs$R2_marginal, 3), " / ", round(r2_obs$R2_conditional, 3)),
>     own_beta  = NA_real_,
>     own_p     = paste0(round(r2_own$R2_marginal, 3), " / ", round(r2_own$R2_conditional, 3))
>   )
>   bind_rows(header_row, data_rows, r2_row)
> }
> combined_table <- bind_rows(
>   make_block(model_obs_reliving_final,  model_own_reliving_final,  "Reliving"),
>   make_block(model_obs_rehearsal_final, model_own_rehearsal_final, "Rehearsal"),
>   make_block(model_obs_intensity_final, model_own_intensity_final, "Intensity")
> )
> knitr::kable(combined_table, na = "",
>   col.names = c("Predictor", "Beta", "p", "Beta", "p"),
>   caption = "Table S8. Memory Characteristics Models: Observer and Own-Eyes Perspectives (final random-slope models with within/between-person decomposition). The R2m/R2c row reports marginal/conditional R2 for the full model in the 'p' column, not a p-value.")

|Predictor                             |   Beta|p             |   Beta|p             |
|:-------------------------------------|------:|:-------------|------:|:-------------|
|Reliving                              |     NA|NA            |     NA|NA            |
|Log(Memory Age)                       |  0.128|<.001         | -0.111|<.001         |

> sd_low_col  <- "#7B2D8B"
> sd_mean_col <- "#2D9B6F"
> sd_high_col <- "#C0392B"
> label_sd_groups <- function(pred_obj) {
>   as.data.frame(pred_obj) %>%
>     mutate(Level = case_when(
>       as.numeric(as.character(group)) < 0 ~ "-1 SD",
>       as.numeric(as.character(group)) > 0 ~ "+1 SD",
>       TRUE                                ~ "Mean"
>     ),
>     Level = factor(Level, levels = c("-1 SD", "Mean", "+1 SD")))
> }
> get_legend_safe <- function(gg_plot) {
>   g <- ggplotGrob(gg_plot)
>   idx <- which(sapply(g$grobs, function(x) x$name) %in% c("guide-box", "guide-box-top"))
>   if (length(idx) == 0) return(NULL)
>   g$grobs[[idx[1]]]
> }
> make_int_plot <- function(df, title, y_label) {
>   ggplot(df, aes(x = x, y = predicted, color = Level, fill = Level)) +
>     geom_ribbon(aes(ymin = conf.low, ymax = conf.high),
>                 alpha = 0.15, color = NA, show.legend = FALSE) +
>     geom_line(linewidth = 1) +
>     scale_color_manual(values = c("-1 SD" = sd_low_col,
>                                   "Mean"  = sd_mean_col,
>                                   "+1 SD" = sd_high_col)) +
>     scale_fill_manual(values  = c("-1 SD" = sd_low_col,
>                                   "Mean"  = sd_mean_col,
>                                   "+1 SD" = sd_high_col),
>                        guide = "none") +
>     scale_y_continuous(limits = c(1, 7), breaks = 1:7) +
>     labs(title  = title,
>          x      = "Log Memory Age (centered)",
>          y      = y_label,
>          color  = NULL) +
>     theme_classic(base_size = 12) +
>     theme(plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5, size = 12),
>           axis.title       = element_text(size = 11),
>           axis.text        = element_text(size = 10),
>           legend.position  = "none",
>           plot.background  = element_rect(fill = "white", color = NA),
>           panel.background = element_rect(fill = "white", color = NA))
> }
> pred_rel_obs <- label_sd_groups(ggpredict(model_obs_reliving_final,
>                   terms = c("LogMemAge_c [all]",
>                             sprintf("Reliving_within [%.4f, 0, %.4f]", -sd_reliving, sd_reliving))))

> compare_coef <- function(full_model, sens_model, label) {
>   cf_full <- coef(summary(full_model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   cf_sens <- coef(summary(sens_model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   data.frame(
>     Outcome = label,
>     Sample  = c("Full (MemAge = 0 recoded to 0.5)", "MemAge = 0 excluded"),
>     beta    = round(c(cf_full["Estimate"], cf_sens["Estimate"]), 4),
>     SE      = round(c(cf_full["Std. Error"], cf_sens["Std. Error"]), 4),
>     p       = c(
>       ifelse(cf_full["Pr(>|t|)"] < .001, "<.001", as.character(round(cf_full["Pr(>|t|)"], 3))),
>       ifelse(cf_sens["Pr(>|t|)"] < .001, "<.001", as.character(round(cf_sens["Pr(>|t|)"], 3)))
>     )
>   )
> }
> sens_table <- bind_rows(
>   compare_coef(model_log_RS_final, model_log_RS_sens, "Observer"),
>   compare_coef(model2_log_RS_final, model2_log_RS_sens, "Own-Eyes")
> )
> knitr::kable(sens_table, row.names = FALSE,
>   caption = "Supplementary Table: Sensitivity Analysis Excluding MemAge = 0 Memories")

> get_study_row <- function(model, outcome_label, study_label) {
>   cf <- coef(summary(model))["LogMemAge_c", ]
>   data.frame(
>     Outcome = outcome_label,
>     Study   = study_label,
>     beta    = round(cf["Estimate"], 4),

Power Analysis Check

No power analyses were detected.

Power analyses need to contain the following information to be interpretable: the type of power analysis, the statistical test, the software used, sample size, critical alpha criterion, power level, effect size, and an effect size metric. In addition, it is recommended to make sure the power analysis is reproducible (by sharing the code, or a screenshot, of the power analysis), and to provide good arguments for why the study was designed to detect an effect of this size.

For an a-priori power analysis, where the sample size is determined, reporting all information would look like:

An a priori power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that for a Cohen’s d = 0.5, an alpha level of 0.05, and a desired power level of 80% required at least 64 participants in each group.

For a sensitivity power analysis, this sentence would look like:

A sensitivity power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that with 64 participants in each group, and an alpha level of 0.05, a desired power level of 80% was reached for an effect size of d = 0.5.
